📘 主报告：研究论文 / Primary Research

Journal: Vaccine  |  Canonical publication date: 2026-08-20  |  DOI: 10.1016/j.vaccine.2026.129059  |  Date basis: first_publication_date  |  Publication status: Current window  |  Citation: 2026, 91, 129059

武汉祖传多抗原严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2病毒样颗粒可预防omicron亚谱系JN.1变种

Wuhan-ancestral multi-antigen SARS-CoV-2 virus-like particles protect against the omicron sublineage JN.1 variant
作者： Seung-Ji Kim, Ho-Won Kim, Seung-Eun Son, Jin-Ha Song, Sun-Min Ahn, So-Jeong Lim, Ha-Na Park, Hyeon-Jin Sa, Na-Yon Kim, Jung-Joon Kim
摘要中文翻译严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)通过刺突蛋白(S)蛋白的突变持续进化,导致抗原性差异的变异株,如奥密克戎亚系JN.1,显著降低抗病毒药物诱导的中和抗体活性。这些局限性凸显了开发能够诱导多层保护性免疫而非仅依赖S蛋白靶向免疫的抗病毒药物策略的必要性。在此,我们利用杆状病毒双表达系统开发了一种基于武汉系的多抗原病毒样颗粒(VLP)疫苗,该疫苗整合了S、包膜(E)、膜(M)和核衣壳(N)蛋白。小鼠通过肌肉注射进行初次免疫,并通过肌肉注射或鼻腔途径进行加强免疫,评估了体液、黏膜和细胞免疫应答。两种加强方案均诱导了强效的全身IgG反应,而通过鼻腔加强则特异性增强了黏膜IgA反应。VLP免疫小鼠的血清能够有效中和同源武汉系毒株,但对JN.1变异株则未表现出可检测的中和活性。尽管如此,VLP免疫在肺部和全身均诱导了抗原特异性的CD4+和CD8+ T细胞反应。在高度易感的K18-hACE2小鼠模型中,VLP免疫完全防止了病毒在武汉系毒株和JN.1变异株挑战后的感染,并显著抑制了呼吸道组织中的病毒复制。组织病理学分析进一步显示,接种VLP的小鼠肺部仅有轻微的间质性肺病变,并伴有组织化的血管周围淋巴细胞浸润。肺部细胞因子分析还揭示了根据挑战毒株和免疫状态而定的免疫应答模式差异。我们的研究结果表明,基于武汉系的多抗原VLP疫苗能够提供对抗原性差异的变异株的交叉保护,这种保护不能仅通过可检测的血清中和活性来完全解释,提示了整合体液、细胞和局部免疫机制的重要性。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2(严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)通过刺突蛋白(S)蛋白的突变持续进化,导致抗原性差异的变异株,如奥密克戎亚系JN.1,显著降低抗病毒药物诱导的中和抗体活性。
设计与对象
这些局限性凸显了开发能够诱导多层保护性免疫而非仅依赖S蛋白靶向免疫的抗病毒药物策略的必要性。
核心方法
在此,我们利用杆状病毒双表达系统开发了一种基于武汉系的多抗原病毒样颗粒(VLP)疫苗,该疫苗整合了S、包膜(E)、膜(M)和核衣壳(N)蛋白。
主要结果
VLP免疫小鼠的血清能够有效中和同源武汉系毒株,但对JN.1变异株则未表现出可检测的中和活性。
解释与创新
我们的研究结果表明,基于武汉谱系的多抗原VLP 疫苗可以针对抗原上不同的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变体提供交叉保护,这种保护不能仅通过可检测到的血清中和活性来完全解释,这表明整合免疫应答涉及体液、细胞和局部免疫机制的重要性。
科研与公卫意义
在高度易感的K18-hACE2小鼠模型中,VLP免疫完全防止了病毒在武汉系毒株和JN.1变异株挑战后的感染,并显著抑制了呼吸道组织中的病毒复制。
局限与证据强度
尽管如此,VLP免疫在肺部和全身均诱导了抗原特异性的CD4+和CD8+ T细胞反应。

Wuhan-ancestral multi-antigen SARS-CoV-2 virus-like particles protect against the omicron sublineage JN.1 variant

Authors: Seung-Ji Kim, Ho-Won Kim, Seung-Eun Son, Jin-Ha Song, Sun-Min Ahn, So-Jeong Lim, Ha-Na Park, Hyeon-Jin Sa, Na-Yon Kim, Jung-Joon Kim
Original abstract (English)
Severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) continues to evolve through mutations in the spike (S) protein, leading to antigenically divergent variants such as the Omicron sublineage JN.1 that markedly reduce vaccine-induced neutralizing antibody activity. These limitations highlight the need for vaccine strategies that can induce multilayered protective immunity rather than relying solely on S protein-targeted immunity. Here, we developed a Wuhan-lineage-based multi-antigen virus-like particle (VLP) vaccine incorporating S, envelope (E), membrane (M), and nucleocapsid (N) proteins using a baculovirus dual-expression system. Mice were primed intramuscularly and boosted via either the intramuscular or intranasal route, and humoral, mucosal, and cellular immune responses were evaluated. Both vaccination regimens induced robust systemic IgG responses, whereas mucosal IgA responses were selectively enhanced following intranasal boosting. Sera from VLP-immunized mice efficiently neutralized the homologous Wuhan-lineage strain but showed no detectable neutralizing activity against the JN.1 variant. Despite this, VLP immunization elicited antigen-specific CD4 + and CD8 + T cell responses in both pulmonary and systemic compartments. In a highly susceptible K18-hACE2 mouse model, VLP immunization completely prevented mortality following challenge with both the ancestral Wuhan-lineage strain and the JN.1 variant and markedly suppressed viral replication in respiratory tissues. Histopathological analysis further showed minimal interstitial lung lesions with organized perivascular lymphocytic infiltration in vaccinated mice, while pulmonary cytokine analysis revealed qualitatively distinct immune response patterns depending on the challenge strain and vaccination status. Our findings show that a Wuhan-lineage-based multi-antigen VLP vaccine can provide cross-protection against an antigenically divergent SARS-CoV-2 variant that is not fully explained by detectable serum neutralizing activity alone, suggesting the importance of integrated immune responses involving humoral, cellular, and local immune mechanisms.
View research seven-element analysis

Severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) continues to evolve through mutations in the spike (S) protein, leading to antigenically divergent variants such as the Omicron sublineage JN.1 that markedly reduce vaccine-induced neutralizing antibody activity.

TRPV4介导的钙离子内流促进了温度依赖性严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2复制

TRPV4-mediated calcium influx contributes temperature-dependent SARS-CoV-2 replication
作者： Moe Kobayashi, Hatsumi Abe, Hikaruko Sugihara, Nene Kobayashi, Seira Omori, Yasuhiro Yamada, Takeshi Ichinohe
摘要中文翻译鼻腔、下呼吸道及发热状态下的温度各不相同,但这些差异对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2复制的影响尚未得到充分理解。本研究表明,祖系严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2、Delta或Omicron BA.5变异株在37°C条件下复制效率最高。我们发现,瞬时受体电位香草酸受体4(TRPV4)介导的钙离子内流(一种在37°C被激活的热敏阳离子通道)有助于严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的有效复制。此外,钙调磷酸酶抑制剂环孢素A(Cys A)及FDA批准的钙通道阻滞剂马尼地平均可抑制病毒复制,并保护叙利亚仓鼠免受严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 Delta变异株致死性感染。值得注意的是,选择性TRPV4拮抗剂也在感染仓鼠中提供了保护作用。这些结果表明,TRPV4介导的钙离子内流在37°C条件下促进了严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的有效复制,并凸显马尼地平作为抗严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2治疗药物再利用的强大候选。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在探究鼻腔、下呼吸道及发热状态下的温度差异如何影响严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2复制,因这些差异的影响尚未得到充分理解。
设计与对象
研究表明,祖系严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2、Delta或Omicron BA.5变异株在37°C条件下复制效率最高,并以叙利亚仓鼠作为感染模型。
核心方法
研究人员确定TRPV4介导的钙离子内流是一种在37°C被激活的热敏阳离子通道,并通过钙调磷酸酶抑制剂环孢素A(Cys A)及FDA批准的钙通道阻滞剂马尼地平,测试其在严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2复制中的作用。
主要结果
研究发现,TRPV4介导的钙离子内流在37°C条件下促进了严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的有效复制,且环孢素A与马尼地平均可抑制病毒复制,并保护叙利亚仓鼠免受Delta变异株致死性感染。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,TRPV4介导的钙离子内流是严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2在体温下复制的关键因素,并凸显马尼地平作为潜在治疗药物再利用的可能性。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果为理解严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2复制的温度依赖性机制提供了见解,并为靶向人类病毒复制的潜在治疗策略提供了可能。
局限与证据强度
该研究仅限于动物模型,未纳入人体临床数据,但由于在受控环境中直接关联TRPV4活性与病毒复制,证据较为充分。

TRPV4-mediated calcium influx contributes temperature-dependent SARS-CoV-2 replication

Authors: Moe Kobayashi, Hatsumi Abe, Hikaruko Sugihara, Nene Kobayashi, Seira Omori, Yasuhiro Yamada, Takeshi Ichinohe
Original abstract (English)
Temperature varies across the nasal cavity, lower respiratory tract, and during febrile conditions, yet the impact of these differences on SARS-CoV-2 replication remains poorly understood. Here, we show that ancestral SARS-CoV-2, Delta, or Omicron BA.5 variants replicate most efficiently at 37°C. We found that transient receptor potential vanilloid 4 (TRPV4)-mediated calcium influx, a thermosensitive cation channel known to be activated at 37°C, contributes to efficient SARS-CoV-2 replication. In addition, a calcineurin inhibitor, cyclosporine A (Cys A), and manidipine, an FDA-approved calcium channel blocker, both suppressed viral replication and protected Syrian hamsters from lethal infection with the SARS-CoV-2 Delta variant. Notably, a selective TRPV4 antagonist also conferred protection in infected hamsters. These results suggest that TRPV4-mediated calcium influx contributes to efficient SARS-CoV-2 replication at 37°C and highlight manidipine as a strong candidate for repurposing as an anti-SARS-CoV-2 therapeutic.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to investigate how temperature differences across the nasal cavity, lower respiratory tract, and during febrile conditions affect SARS-CoV-2 replication, as the impact of these variations remains poorly understood.
Design and population
The study demonstrated that ancestral SARS-CoV-2, Delta, or Omicron BA.5 variants replicate most efficiently at 37°C, using Syrian hamsters as a model for infection.
Core methods
The researchers identified TRPV4-mediated calcium influx as a thermosensitive cation channel activated at 37°C and tested its role in SARS-CoV-2 replication using a calcineurin inhibitor, cyclosporine A (Cys A), and manidipine, an FDA-approved calcium channel blocker.
Main results
The study found that TRPV4-mediated calcium influx contributes to efficient SARS-CoV-2 replication at 37°C, and both cyclosporine A and manidipine suppressed viral replication and protected Syrian hamsters from lethal infection with the Delta variant.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest that TRPV4-mediated calcium influx is a key factor in SARS-CoV-2 replication at body temperature and highlight manidipine as a potential therapeutic candidate for repurposing.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results provide insights into the temperature-dependent mechanisms of SARS-CoV-2 replication and suggest a possible therapeutic strategy for targeting viral replication in humans.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to animal models and does not include human clinical data, but the evidence is strong due to the direct link between TRPV4 activity and viral replication in a controlled setting.

Effectiveness of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) original vaccines to prevent COVID-19-related hospitalisations among Alaska Native peoples, January 2021–October 2022

Effectiveness of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) original vaccines to prevent COVID-19-related hospitalisations among Alaska Native peoples, January 2021–October 2022
作者： Ian Blake, Rosalyn Singleton, Dana Bruden, Jacob Gray, Marc Fischer, James Keck
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
American Indian/Alaska Native peoples experienced disproportionately high COVID-19 disease burden in the United States, yet there were limited data on vaccine effectiveness in this population, creating a critical knowledge gap.
设计与对象
A test-negative case–control study was conducted using electronic health record data to evaluate the effectiveness of the original three FDA-authorised COVID-19 vaccines in preventing COVID-19-associated hospitalisations among Alaska Native adults during January 2021 to October 2022.
核心方法
The study used electronic health records in a test-negative case–control design to assess vaccine effectiveness against COVID-19-associated hospitalisations, intensive care unit admissions, mechanical ventilation and death.
主要结果
COVID-19 vaccines were effective in preventing COVID-19-associated hospitalisation, intensive care unit admission, mechanical ventilation and death in this population, but vaccine effectiveness waned over time and during the post-Omicron variant period.
解释与创新
作者将研究结果解释为表明加强接种疫苗接种在奥密克戎变异株流行期间仍保持有效性,强调了需要及时接种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种以应对随时间推移有效性下降以及新变异株的出现。
科研与公卫意义
本研究支持在疫情期间利用电子健康记录快速评估疫苗有效性,并强调在高风险人群中接种加强针以维持对重症的保护作用的重要性。
局限与证据强度
本研究依赖电子健康记录可能引入数据完整性和选择偏倚;观察性研究设计限制因果推断;证据强度评估为中等。

Effectiveness of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) original vaccines to prevent COVID-19-related hospitalisations among Alaska Native peoples, January 2021–October 2022

Authors: Ian Blake, Rosalyn Singleton, Dana Bruden, Jacob Gray, Marc Fischer, James Keck
Original abstract (English)
American Indian/Alaska Native (AI/AN) peoples experienced some of the highest rates of coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) disease in the United States. However, there are limited data on COVID-19 vaccine effectiveness in this population. We performed a test-negative case‒control study using electronic health record data to evaluate the effectiveness of the original three FDA-authorised vaccines to prevent COVID-19-associated hospitalisations among AI/AN adults during 2021-2022. COVID-19 vaccines were effective in preventing COVID-19-associated hospitalisation, intensive care unit admission, mechanical ventilation and death in this population. However, vaccine effectiveness waned over time and during the post-Omicron variant time period. Booster vaccination proved effective during a period when the Omicron variant was circulating. Waning effectiveness with time and the introduction of new variants supports the need to stay up-to-date with SARS-CoV-2 vaccination. Evaluating vaccine effectiveness using electronic health records proved to be a useful tool during a pandemic.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
American Indian/Alaska Native peoples experienced disproportionately high COVID-19 disease burden in the United States, yet there were limited data on vaccine effectiveness in this population, creating a critical knowledge gap.
Design and population
A test-negative case–control study was conducted using electronic health record data to evaluate the effectiveness of the original three FDA-authorised COVID-19 vaccines in preventing COVID-19-associated hospitalisations among Alaska Native adults during January 2021 to October 2022.
Core methods
The study used electronic health records in a test-negative case–control design to assess vaccine effectiveness against COVID-19-associated hospitalisations, intensive care unit admissions, mechanical ventilation and death.
Main results
COVID-19 vaccines were effective in preventing COVID-19-associated hospitalisation, intensive care unit admission, mechanical ventilation and death in this population, but vaccine effectiveness waned over time and during the post-Omicron variant period.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted the findings as demonstrating that booster vaccination remained effective during Omicron circulation, highlighting the need to stay up-to-date with SARS-CoV-2 vaccination as effectiveness wanes with time and new variants emerge.
Scientific and public-health significance
The study supports the use of electronic health records for rapid vaccine effectiveness evaluation during pandemics and underscores the importance of booster doses to maintain protection against severe outcomes in high-risk populations.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study’s reliance on electronic health records may introduce data completeness and selection biases, and the observational design limits causal inference; the evidence strength is assessed as moderate.

在气液界面人肺类器官芯片中重现严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染

Recapitulating SARS-CoV-2 Infection in a Human Lung Organoid-on-a-Chip at the Air-Liquid Interface
作者： Sohyun Park, So-Hui Kim, Bokyong Kim, Eun-Young Eo, Hyung-Jun Kim, Yuhui Jeong, Jeongbin Park, Young-Jae Cho
摘要中文翻译摘要 背景 慢性呼吸系统疾病及新发病原体(如严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2)是全球主要的健康负担。然而,传统体外模型难以重现人肺的功能复杂性,限制了机制研究与治疗开发。方法 为解决这一问题,本研究构建了一种肺类器官芯片,其中患者来源的气道类器官在气液界面分化,并与微流控平台上的血管内皮细胞和巨噬细胞共培养。该芯片被用于感染四种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株——原始株(Wuhan)、Delta、Omicron BA.1及Omicron XBB.1——并通过单细胞RNA测序进行表征。结果 该平台在气液界面重建了原生气道细胞组成,单细胞表征显示出显著的上皮重塑,包括纤毛细胞丢失、干扰素应答受抑以及部分上皮-间充质转化启动。同时伴随内皮细胞血管生成反应,表现为扩增的血管生成尖端样状态及血管内皮生长因子(VEGF)非依赖性血管生成配体程序,且上述反应在四种变异株中保持一致。结论 综合而言,该肺类器官芯片重现了保守的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2上皮与血管反应,为呼吸系统疾病研究提供了一种生理相关的平台。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在构建更精确的人肺体外模型,以更好地理解严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染及其对呼吸组织的影响。
设计与对象
本研究构建了一种肺类器官芯片模型,采用患者来源的气道类器官与血管内皮细胞和巨噬细胞共培养。该模型被用于感染四种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株:原始株(Wuhan)、Delta、Omicron BA.1及Omicron XBB.1。
核心方法
肺类器官芯片在气液界面分化,并感染四种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株。随后通过单细胞RNA测序对感染后的细胞反应进行表征。
主要结果
单细胞RNA测序显示出显著的上皮重塑,包括纤毛细胞丢失、干扰素应答受抑以及部分上皮-间充质转化启动。内皮细胞血管生成反应表现为扩增的血管生成尖端样状态及血管内皮生长因子(VEGF)非依赖性血管生成配体程序,且上述反应在四种变异株中保持一致。
解释与创新
作者将这些发现解释为证明肺类器官芯片模型有效重现了保守的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2上皮与血管反应,并提供了一种生理相关的平台用于研究呼吸系统疾病机制。
科研与公卫意义
该发现为呼吸系统疾病研究提供了有价值的工具,使更精确地研究严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染及其对肺组织的影响成为可能,并有望为未来治疗开发与机制研究提供参考。
局限与证据强度
本研究仅限于体外观察,未考虑体内复杂性。然而,证据较为充分,因其直接报道了该平台在多种变异株中重现严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2反应的能力。

Recapitulating SARS-CoV-2 Infection in a Human Lung Organoid-on-a-Chip at the Air-Liquid Interface

Authors: Sohyun Park, So-Hui Kim, Bokyong Kim, Eun-Young Eo, Hyung-Jun Kim, Yuhui Jeong, Jeongbin Park, Young-Jae Cho
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Chronic respiratory diseases and emerging pathogens such as SARS-CoV-2 are a major global health burden. Traditional in vitro models, however, poorly recapitulate the functional complexity of the human lung, limiting mechanistic study and therapeutic development. Methods To address this, we developed a lung organoid-on-a-chip in which patient-derived airway organoids are differentiated at an air–liquid interface and co-cultured with vascular endothelial cells and macrophages on a microfluidic platform. The chip was infected with four SARS-CoV-2 variants - Wuhan, Delta, Omicron BA.1, and Omicron XBB.1 - and profiled by single-cell RNA sequencing. Results The platform reconstituted native airway cellular composition within an air–liquid interface, and single-cell profiling revealed pronounced epithelial remodeling, including loss of ciliated cells, blunted interferon responses, and partial epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition priming. This was accompanied by an endothelial angiogenic response marked by an expanded angiogenic tip-like state and a VEGF-independent angiogenic-ligand program, with these responses remaining consistent across all four variants. Conclusions Collectively, this lung organoid-on-a-chip recapitulates conserved SARS-CoV-2 epithelial and vascular responses, providing a physiologically relevant platform for respiratory disease research.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to develop a more accurate in vitro model of the human lung to better understand SARS-CoV-2 infection and its effects on respiratory tissues.
Design and population
The study involved the creation of a lung organoid-on-a-chip model using patient-derived airway organoids co-cultured with vascular endothelial cells and macrophages. The model was infected with four SARS-CoV-2 variants: Wuhan, Delta, Omicron BA.1, and Omicron XBB.1.
Core methods
The lung organoid-on-a-chip was differentiated at an air–liquid interface and infected with four SARS-CoV-2 variants. Single-cell RNA sequencing was used to profile the cellular responses to infection.
Main results
Single-cell RNA sequencing revealed pronounced epithelial remodeling, including loss of ciliated cells, blunted interferon responses, and partial epithelial-to-mesenchymal transition priming. The endothelial angiogenic response was marked by an expanded angiogenic tip-like state and a VEGF-independent angiogenic-ligand program, with these responses remaining consistent across all four variants.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted these findings as demonstrating that the lung organoid-on-a-chip model effectively recapitulates conserved SARS-CoV-2 epithelial and vascular responses, offering a physiologically relevant platform for studying respiratory disease mechanisms.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings provide a valuable tool for respiratory disease research, enabling more accurate study of SARS-CoV-2 infection and its impact on lung tissues, which could inform future therapeutic development and mechanistic studies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to in vitro observations and does not account for in vivo complexities. However, the evidence is strong as it directly reports on the platform's ability to recapitulate SARS-CoV-2 responses across multiple variants.

Missed Nursing Care in Pandemic Crisis of COVID-19: A Systematic Review.

Missed Nursing Care in Pandemic Crisis of COVID-19: A Systematic Review.
作者： Mahsa Pourshaban, Hadi Hasankhani, Atefeh Allahbakhshian, Sarieh Poortaghi
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study aimed to address the issue of missed nursing care (MNC) during the COVID-19 pandemic, highlighting the risks it poses to patient safety and the need for improved care practices.
设计与对象
本研究为系统性综述,系统检索了包括 ProQuest、CINAHL、Web of Science 及 PubMed 在内的多个数据库,检索时间为 2019 年 12 月至 2024 年 7 月。综述纳入了来自 17 个国家的 31 项定量研究。
核心方法
本研究遵循 PRISMA 指南,并基于 PICOT-SD 标准使用布尔运算符进行系统检索。关键词选自医学主题词(MeSH)及既往研究。
主要结果
错失护理要素依据护理干预分类(NIC)被归入 5 个领域,其中最常见的要素出现在基础生理领域。影响因素被组织为 6 个层级,高护理需求与人员配置不足被确定为主要问题。
解释与创新
作者解读认为,大流行凸显了护士因多种原因不愿与患者进行近距离接触,导致护理显著流失。研究强调未来应对卫生危机时需采用有效管理策略。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果为制定针对性策略以管理大流行期间的错失护理提供依据,从而提升患者安全与高需求临床环境中的护理质量。
局限与证据强度
本研究受限于对已发表定量研究的依赖,可能无法涵盖错失护理的所有方面。由于纳入研究均为观察性研究,证据强度为中等。

Missed Nursing Care in Pandemic Crisis of COVID-19: A Systematic Review.

Authors: Mahsa Pourshaban, Hadi Hasankhani, Atefeh Allahbakhshian, Sarieh Poortaghi
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND AND OBJECTIVE: High-quality nursing care ensures patient safety. However, missed nursing care (MNC) poses a significant risk. During the COVID-19 pandemic, the dynamic clinical environment negatively impacted nurses and patient care quality. This review examined the most frequently reported elements and contributing factors of MNCs in studies conducted during the pandemic. METHODS: This was a systematic review (PROSPERO CRD42023460938). A systematic search was conducted across databases including ProQuest, CINAHL, Web of Science, Google Scholar, Scopus, PubMed, PROSPERO, and Cochrane CENTRAL, using Boolean operators based on PICOT-SD criteria from December 2019 to July 2024. Keywords were selected based on Medical Subject Headings (MeSH) and previous studies. After reviewing the inclusion and exclusion criteria, 31 quantitative studies from 17 countries were included in the final evaluation. Findings were reported following PRISMA guidelines. RESULTS: During the pandemic, MNC elements were categorized into 5 domains based on the Nursing Intervention Classification (NIC). At the same time, contributing factors were organized into 6 levels based on the MNC model. The most frequently observed MNC elements were identified in the basic physiological domain. The primary contributing factor for MNCs was the demand for patient care, with inadequate staffing being the most reported issue during the pandemic. CONCLUSION: The SARS-CoV-2 pandemic revealed that nurses' reluctance to engage in close contact with patients for various reasons led to significant losses in care. High care demands and staffing shortages remain prevalent factors contributing to MNCs, highlighting the need for effective management strategies in future health crises.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to address the issue of missed nursing care (MNC) during the COVID-19 pandemic, highlighting the risks it poses to patient safety and the need for improved care practices.
Design and population
This was a systematic review conducted across multiple databases, including ProQuest, CINAHL, Web of Science, and PubMed, from December 2019 to July 2024. The review included 31 quantitative studies from 17 countries.
Core methods
The study followed PRISMA guidelines and used Boolean operators based on PICOT-SD criteria for systematic searching. Keywords were selected based on Medical Subject Headings (MeSH) and previous studies.
Main results
MNC elements were categorized into 5 domains based on the Nursing Intervention Classification (NIC), with the most frequently observed elements in the basic physiological domain. Contributing factors were organized into 6 levels, with high care demands and inadequate staffing identified as the primary issues.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that the pandemic highlighted the impact of nurses' reluctance to engage in close patient contact, leading to significant losses in care. They emphasized the need for effective management strategies in future health crises.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings inform the development of targeted strategies to manage missed nursing care during pandemics, improving patient safety and care quality in high-demand clinical settings.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited by its reliance on published quantitative studies, which may not capture all aspects of MNC. The evidence is moderate in strength due to the observational nature of the included studies.

一种人源信息驱动的多区段T细胞疫苗诱导广泛CD8+免疫并保护对抗严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染

A human-informed multi-patch T-cell vaccine induces broad CD8+ immunity and protects against SARS-CoV-2 infection
作者： Laura Sin, Laura Marcos-Villar, Beatriz Perdiguero, Enrique Álvarez, Sara Flores, Carlos Óscar S. Sorzano, Daniel del Hoyo, Philipp Lapuhs, Alonso MJ, Esteban M
摘要中文翻译摘要 新型冠状病毒感染变异株的出现凸显了对疫苗的需求,其通过靶向保守的T细胞应答补充刺突蛋白导向的免疫。本研究开发了CoV2-TMEPu,一种人源信息驱动的多区段免疫原,将来自严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2结构蛋白、辅助蛋白和复制相关蛋白的保守且天然免疫显性区域整合为单一mRNA-脂质纳米颗粒(LNP)疫苗。LNP-CoV2-TMEPu在体外高效表达并诱导早期固有免疫激活。同源初免-加强免疫诱导了强效、持久且多功能的CD8+ T细胞应答,同时伴有低强度CD4+应答。表位作图显示跨多种病毒蛋白的广泛但分层组织的多特异性识别及多样化的CD8+记忆表型。疫苗接种还诱导了针对抗原性差异显著的Omicron衍生变异株的交叉反应性中和活性,并保护K18-hACE2小鼠免受致死性新型冠状病毒感染攻击,降低了疾病严重程度和病毒载量。综上,这些发现确立了人源信息驱动的多区段免疫原设计作为下一代变异株韧性冠状病毒疫苗的有前景策略。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株的出现凸显了对疫苗的需求,这些疫苗能够通过靶向保守的T细胞应答来补充针对刺突蛋白的免疫。
设计与对象
本研究开发了CoV2-TMEPu,一种人源信息驱动的多区段免疫原,将来自严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2结构蛋白、辅助蛋白和复制相关蛋白的保守且天然免疫显性区域整合为单一mRNA-脂质纳米颗粒(LNP)疫苗。
核心方法
LNP-CoV2-TMEPu在体外高效表达并诱导早期固有免疫激活。
主要结果
同源初免-加强免疫诱导了强效、持久且多功能的CD8+ T细胞应答,同时伴有低强度CD4+应答。
解释与创新
表位作图显示跨多种病毒蛋白的广泛但分层组织的多特异性识别及多样化的CD8+记忆表型。
科研与公卫意义
疫苗接种 还诱导了针对抗原性不同的奥密克戎衍生变异株的交叉反应性中和活性,并保护了 K18-hACE2 小鼠免受致死性 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 攻击,降低了疾病严重程度和病毒载量。
局限与证据强度
综上,这些发现确立了人源信息驱动的多区段免疫原设计作为下一代变异株韧性冠状病毒疫苗的有前景策略。

A human-informed multi-patch T-cell vaccine induces broad CD8+ immunity and protects against SARS-CoV-2 infection

Authors: Laura Sin, Laura Marcos-Villar, Beatriz Perdiguero, Enrique Álvarez, Sara Flores, Carlos Óscar S. Sorzano, Daniel del Hoyo, Philipp Lapuhs, Alonso MJ, Esteban M
Original abstract (English)
Abstract The emergence of SARS-CoV-2 variants has highlighted the need for vaccines that complement Spike-directed immunity by targeting conserved T-cell responses. Here, we developed CoV2-TMEPu, a human-informed multi-patch immunogen integrating conserved and naturally immunodominant regions from structural, accessory and replication-associated SARS-CoV-2 proteins into a single mRNA-lipid nanoparticle (LNP) vaccine. LNP-CoV2-TMEPu was efficiently expressed in vitro and induced early innate immune activation. Homologous prime-boost immunization elicited robust, durable and polyfunctional CD8+ T-cell responses together with lower-magnitude CD4+ responses. Epitope mapping revealed broad but hierarchically organized multispecific recognition spanning multiple viral proteins and a diversified CD8+ memory phenotype. Vaccination also induced cross-reactive neutralizing activity against antigenically distinct Omicron-derived variants and protected K18-hACE2 mice from a lethal SARS-CoV-2 challenge, reducing disease severity and viral burden. Together, these findings establish human-informed multi-patch immunogen design as a promising strategy for next-generation variant-resilient coronavirus vaccines.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The emergence of SARS-CoV-2 variants has highlighted the need for vaccines that complement Spike-directed immunity by targeting conserved T-cell responses.
Design and population
Here, we developed CoV2-TMEPu, a human-informed multi-patch immunogen integrating conserved and naturally immunodominant regions from structural, accessory and replication-associated SARS-CoV-2 proteins into a single mRNA-lipid nanoparticle (LNP) vaccine.
Core methods
LNP-CoV2-TMEPu was efficiently expressed in vitro and induced early innate immune activation.
Main results
Homologous prime-boost immunization elicited robust, durable and polyfunctional CD8+ T-cell responses together with lower-magnitude CD4+ responses.
Interpretation and novelty
Epitope mapping revealed broad but hierarchically organized multispecific recognition spanning multiple viral proteins and a diversified CD8+ memory phenotype.
Scientific and public-health significance
Vaccination also induced cross-reactive neutralizing activity against antigenically distinct Omicron-derived variants and protected K18-hACE2 mice from a lethal SARS-CoV-2 challenge, reducing disease severity and viral burden.
Limitations and evidence strength
Together, these findings establish human-informed multi-patch immunogen design as a promising strategy for next-generation variant-resilient coronavirus vaccines.

急性感染期肠道微生物组特征与长新冠相关

Gut microbiome signatures during acute infection are associated with long COVID.
作者： Isin Y Comba, Ruben A T Mars, Lu Yang, Mitchell Dumais, Jun Chen, Trena M. Van Gorp, Jonathan J. Harrington, Jason P. Sinnwell, Stephen Johnson, LaRinda A. Holland
摘要中文翻译背景:新型冠状病毒感染后,10%-30%的非住院患者会出现长新冠(LC)表现,导致显著发病率。急性感染期肠道微生物组组成在长新冠发展中的预测作用尚不明确,部分原因在于该疾病的异质性。目的:确定急性期新型冠状病毒感染感染者的肠道微生物组组成是否可预测后续长新冠,并探索微生物组特征在疾病亚表型中的作用。设计:我们开展了一项纵向队列研究,纳入799名因类似症状表现而接受新型冠状病毒感染检测的门诊患者,其中380名新型冠状病毒感染阳性者和419名阴性者。通过宏基因组测序收集两个时间点的粪便样本。采用L1正则化逻辑回归基于微生物组和临床元数据预测长新冠。结果:与完全康复者及症状相似的未感染对照组相比,发展为长新冠的个体在急性感染期拥有截然不同的肠道微生物组。然而,急性期(0-1个月)与亚急性期(1-2个月)之间的肠道微生物组时间变化在三组中相似。通过机器学习,我们证明肠道微生物组对后续长新冠具有一定预测信号,但模型性能不足以用于临床预测,这可能反映了长新冠的异质性。最后,我们识别出四个长新冠症状簇,其中胃肠道症状和仅疲劳症状组与肠道微生物组改变高度相关。结论:肠道微生物组有望为理解长新冠的异质性提供解决方案。更大规模的纵向队列研究及表型感知计算算法可能有助于克服当前模型性能限制,并支持靶向诊断和治疗策略的开发。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Long COVID (LC) manifests in 10%-30% of non-hospitalized individuals post-SARS-CoV-2 infection, leading to significant morbidity. The predictive role of gut microbiome composition during acute infection in the development of LC is not well understood, partly because of the heterogeneous nature of the disease.
设计与对象
我们开展了一项纵向 队列研究 研究,纳入了 799 名门诊参与者,因症状表现相似接受 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 检测,其中 380 名为 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 阳性个体,419 名为阴性个体。
核心方法
通过宏基因组测序收集两个时间点的粪便样本。采用L1正则化逻辑回归基于微生物组和临床元数据预测长新冠。
主要结果
与完全康复者及症状相似的未感染对照组相比,发展为长新冠的个体在急性感染期拥有截然不同的肠道微生物组。然而,急性期(0-1个月)与亚急性期(1-2个月)之间的肠道微生物组时间变化在三组中相似。
解释与创新
肠道微生物组有望为理解长新冠的异质性提供解决方案。通过机器学习,我们证明肠道微生物组对后续长新冠具有一定预测信号,但模型性能不足以用于临床预测,这可能反映了长新冠的异质性。
科研与公卫意义
研究结果可能为未来探索肠道微生物组在长新冠中的作用提供依据,并支持靶向诊断和治疗策略的开发。更大规模的纵向队列研究及表型感知计算算法可能有助于克服当前模型性能限制,并支持靶向诊断和治疗策略的开发。
局限与证据强度
该研究受限于长新冠的异质性,这可能影响微生物组的预测能力。

Gut microbiome signatures during acute infection are associated with long COVID.

Authors: Isin Y Comba, Ruben A T Mars, Lu Yang, Mitchell Dumais, Jun Chen, Trena M. Van Gorp, Jonathan J. Harrington, Jason P. Sinnwell, Stephen Johnson, LaRinda A. Holland
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: Long COVID (LC) manifests in 10%-30% of non-hospitalized individuals post-SARS-CoV-2 infection, leading to significant morbidity. The predictive role of gut microbiome composition during acute infection in the development of LC is not well understood, partly because of the heterogeneous nature of the disease. OBJECTIVES: To determine whether the gut microbiome composition in the acute phase of SARS-CoV-2 infection predicts subsequent LC and to investigate the role of microbiome signatures in disease subphenotypes. DESIGN: We conducted a longitudinal cohort study involving 799 outpatient participants tested for SARS-CoV-2 due to similar symptom presentation, including 380 SARS-CoV-2 positive and 419 negative individuals. Stool samples were collected at two time points for metagenomic sequencing. Logistic regression with L1 regularization was employed to predict LC based on the microbiome and clinical metadata. RESULTS: The individuals who developed LC harbored a distinct gut microbiome during acute infection compared to those who recovered fully and uninfected controls with similar symptomatology. However, the temporal changes in the gut microbiome between the acute (0-1 month) and post-acute (1-2 months) phases were similar across the three cohorts. Using machine learning, we showed that the gut microbiome carried a modest signal for subsequent LC, but model performance was insufficient for clinical prediction, likely reflecting the heterogeneous nature of LC. Finally, we identified four LC symptom clusters, with gastrointestinal and fatigue-only groups strongly linked to gut microbiome alterations. CONCLUSION: The gut microbiome can potentially offer solutions for understanding the heterogeneous nature of LC. Larger cohorts and phenotype-aware computational algorithms may help overcome current model performance limitations and support the development of targeted diagnostic and therapeutic strategies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background

英格兰在和后续疫苗接种推广期间,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染后患有严重精神疾病人群的死亡率不平等:一项回顾性全国范围关联队列研究研究

Inequalities in mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection for people with severe mental illness during and following vaccination roll-out in England: a retrospective, whole-country linked cohort study.
作者： Natasha Chilman, Laia Bécares, Alexandru Dregan, Amy Ronaldson, Jadene Lewis, Mehrdad A Mizani, Jayati Das-Munshi, CVD-COVID-UK Consortium
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
People with severe mental illness (SMI) had a higher risk of death following SARS-CoV-2 infection compared with people without SMI during the COVID-19 pandemic peak. It is unclear whether this risk reduced in later stages after vaccination roll-out.
设计与对象
This retrospective, whole-country cohort study analyzed linked electronic health records for all people in England registered to primary care who were alive in November, 2019. The study population included people who had COVID-19 between Jan 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with information on deaths and vaccination.
核心方法
Data were extracted from the NHS England Secure Data Environment provided through the British Heart Foundation Data Science Centre CVD-COVID-UK-COVID-IMPACT Consortium linked datasets, which were curated to form a dataset in July, 2024. Cox regression was used to estimate differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality.
主要结果
13 463 945 people with COVID-19 were identified, of whom 160 190 (1·19%) had SMI. People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio [aHR] 1·56, 95% CI 1·53-1·59). All-cause mortality risk in people with SMI was elevated in stage 1 (aHR 1·27, 95% CI 1·22-1·32), but larger in the stage 2 (aHR 1·56, 1·51-1·61) and stage 3 (aHR 1·55, 1·50-1·59).
解释与创新
Excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the COVID-19 pandemic. Higher COVID-19 mortality risk in people with SMI persisted but was partially attenuated by vaccination (aHR 1·25, 1·17-1·34).
科研与公卫意义
需要采取预防性公共卫生和临床策略,以应对患有严重精神疾病(SMI)人群中 死亡率 的过度发生并降低 疫苗接种 发生率。研究结果凸显了针对该脆弱人群采取有针对性干预措施以减少 死亡率 风险的必要性。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于关联性电子健康记录,可能在数据完整性和准确性方面存在局限性。由于回顾性研究的性质及潜在未测量混杂因素,证据强度为中等。

Inequalities in mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection for people with severe mental illness during and following vaccination roll-out in England: a retrospective, whole-country linked cohort study.

Authors: Natasha Chilman, Laia Bécares, Alexandru Dregan, Amy Ronaldson, Jadene Lewis, Mehrdad A Mizani, Jayati Das-Munshi, CVD-COVID-UK Consortium
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: People with severe mental illness (SMI) had a higher risk of death following SARS-CoV-2 infection compared with people without SMI during the COVID-19 pandemic peak. It is unclear whether this risk reduced in later stages after vaccination roll-out. We aimed to assess all-cause and cause-specific mortality following SARS-CoV-2 infection in people with or without SMI across pandemic stages, using whole-country data, while accounting for sociodemographic and clinical characteristics. METHODS: In this retrospective, whole-country cohort study, we analysed linked electronic health records for all people in England registered to primary care who were alive in November, 2019 (>99% of the population). The study population included people who had COVID-19 between Jan 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with information on deaths and vaccination. Data extracted from the NHS England Secure Data Environment provided through the British Heart Foundation Data Science Centre CVD-COVID-UK-COVID-IMPACT Consortium linked datasets were curated to form a dataset in July, 2024. Cox regression estimated differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality. Analyses were stratified by pandemic stages, defined by WHO policy and UK Government vaccination roll-out: Jan 31-Sept 30, 2020 (stage 1); Oct 1, 2020-July 18, 2021 (stage 2, vaccination roll-out); and July 19, 2021-June 30, 2023 (stage 3, post-vaccination roll-out). SMI comprised schizophrenia, schizoaffective disorder, bipolar disorder, or other affective and non-affective disorder with psychosis. A patient and public involvement panel was consulted during design phases of the study. FINDINGS: 13 463 945 people with COVID-19 were identified, of whom 160 190 (1·19%) had SMI. People with SMI were older [mean age [SD], SMI 49·7 years [17·9], non-SMI 45·0 years [17·6]), had a similar sex split (females SMI vs non-SMI: 56·3% vs 56·6%); and were mostly White British (77·3% or 76·3%). People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio [aHR] 1·56, 95% CI 1·53-1·59). All-cause mortality risk in people with SMI was elevated in stage 1 (aHR 1·27, 95% CI 1·22-1·32), but larger in the stage 2 (aHR 1·56, 1·51-1·61) and stage 3 (aHR 1·55, 1·50-1·59). Vaccination prevalence was initially higher for people with SMI (in stage 2: 112 795 [73·2%, 95% CI 72·9-73·4], compared with people without SMI, 8 852 970 [67·4%, 67·3-67·4]). By study end, fewer people with SMI were fully vaccinated (127 145 [79·4%, 79·2-79·6]), compared with people without SMI (11 605 915 [87·2%, 87·2-87·3]. Higher COVID-19 mortality risk in people with SMI persisted but was partially attenuated by vaccination (aHR 1·25, 1·17-1·34). INTERPRETATION: Excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the COVID-19 pandemic. Preventive public health and clinical strategies are needed to address excess mortality and lower vaccination. FUNDING: Health Foundation-Academy of Medical Sciences.

Question and background
People with severe mental illness (SMI) had a higher risk of death following SARS-CoV-2 infection compared with people without SMI during the COVID-19 pandemic peak. It is unclear whether this risk reduced in later stages after vaccination roll-out.
Design and population
This retrospective, whole-country cohort study analyzed linked electronic health records for all people in England registered to primary care who were alive in November, 2019. The study population included people who had COVID-19 between Jan 31, 2020, and June 30, 2023, with information on deaths and vaccination.
Core methods
Data were extracted from the NHS England Secure Data Environment provided through the British Heart Foundation Data Science Centre CVD-COVID-UK-COVID-IMPACT Consortium linked datasets, which were curated to form a dataset in July, 2024. Cox regression was used to estimate differences in all-cause and COVID-19 mortality.
Main results
13 463 945 people with COVID-19 were identified, of whom 160 190 (1·19%) had SMI. People with SMI had higher all-cause mortality than those without (adjusted hazard ratio [aHR] 1·56, 95% CI 1·53-1·59). All-cause mortality risk in people with SMI was elevated in stage 1 (aHR 1·27, 95% CI 1·22-1·32), but larger in the stage 2 (aHR 1·56, 1·51-1·61) and stage 3 (aHR 1·55, 1·50-1·59).
Interpretation and novelty
Excess mortality in people with SMI persisted throughout the COVID-19 pandemic. Higher COVID-19 mortality risk in people with SMI persisted but was partially attenuated by vaccination (aHR 1·25, 1·17-1·34).
Scientific and public-health significance
Preventive public health and clinical strategies are needed to address excess mortality and lower vaccination rates among people with SMI. The findings highlight the need for targeted interventions to reduce mortality risk in this vulnerable group.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on linked electronic health records, which may have limitations in data completeness and accuracy. The evidence is moderate in strength due to the retrospective nature and potential for unmeasured confounding.

Myelin Oligodendrocyte Glycoprotein (MOG) Antibody-Associated Disease Presenting as Acute Disseminated Encephalomyelitis Following COVID-19 mRNA Vaccination

Myelin Oligodendrocyte Glycoprotein (MOG) Antibody-Associated Disease Presenting as Acute Disseminated Encephalomyelitis Following COVID-19 mRNA Vaccination
作者： Monica He, Christopher Bondoc, Robert Fekete
摘要中文翻译

问题与背景
This study aimed to report a case of autoimmune demyelinating disease presenting as ADEM following COVID-19 mRNA vaccination, highlighting the potential association between the vaccine and such rare neurological events.
设计与对象
A single-case study was conducted on a 53-year-old Asian-American female patient with a history of obstructive sleep apnea and no prior autoimmune disease, who presented with neurological symptoms following a three-month history of headaches after receiving her first dose of the Pfizer COVID-19 vaccine.

患者接受了脑部影像学检查和脑脊液(CSF)分析。在出院后6个月,使用活细胞基质法对血清和CSF样本进行了抗MOG抗体检测。

Myelin Oligodendrocyte Glycoprotein (MOG) Antibody-Associated Disease Presenting as Acute Disseminated Encephalomyelitis Following COVID-19 mRNA Vaccination

Authors: Monica He, Christopher Bondoc, Robert Fekete
Original abstract (English)
Acute disseminated encephalomyelitis (ADEM) is a rapidly progressive autoimmune demyelinating condition that primarily affects the central nervous system. The exact pathogenesis is not well understood, but current literature suggests roles for both environmental triggers and genetic predispositions. Myelin oligodendrocyte glycoprotein antibody-associated disease (MOGAD) is an increasingly recognized autoimmune demyelinating disorder that can present with an ADEM-like phenotype, particularly in adults. We report the case of a 53-year-old Asian-American female patient of Taiwanese ancestry with a past medical history of obstructive sleep apnea and no prior autoimmune disease or family history of demyelinating disorders who presented to the emergency room (ER) with left-sided numbness and weakness, ataxia, vertigo, and slurred speech in the context of a three-month long history of headaches that started one day after she received her first dose of Pfizer COVID-19 vaccine. Brain imaging studies showed findings most suggestive of ADEM. Initial pre-treatment cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) analysis showed markedly elevated protein (473 mg/dL, reference 15-45 mg/dL) with a normal glucose level and mild pleocytosis (6 WBC/μL). Extensive workup was negative for infectious, oncologic, and autoimmune etiologies including negative serum and CSF anti-MOG antibody testing during the acute presentation. The initial treatment of a five-day intravenous methylprednisolone course (250 mg every six hours, total 1,000 mg/day) did not result in significant clinical improvement. The patient was then started on plasma exchange therapy and successfully recovered by the end of the last session with objective improvement in ambulation, dysarthria, and limb strength. The patient tested positive for anti-myelin oligodendrocyte glycoprotein antibody in serum six months after hospital discharge at a titer of 1:32 by live cell-based assay. This case provides an example of a rare but serious event temporally associated with the SARS-CoV-19 mRNA vaccine and MOGAD presenting as ADEM in a previously healthy adult. The absolute risk of demyelinating events following COVID-19 vaccination is extremely low, and the benefit of vaccination vastly outweighs these rare risks.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
This study aimed to report a case of autoimmune demyelinating disease presenting as ADEM following COVID-19 mRNA vaccination, highlighting the potential association between the vaccine and such rare neurological events.
Design and population
A single-case study was conducted on a 53-year-old Asian-American female patient with a history of obstructive sleep apnea and no prior autoimmune disease, who presented with neurological symptoms following a three-month history of headaches after receiving her first dose of the Pfizer COVID-19 vaccine.
Core methods
The patient underwent brain imaging and cerebrospinal fluid (CSF) analysis, and anti-MOG antibody testing was performed on serum and CSF samples six months after hospital discharge using a live cell-based assay.
Main results
Brain imaging suggested ADEM, and CSF analysis showed markedly elevated protein levels with mild pleocytosis. The patient tested positive for anti-MOG antibodies at a titer of 1:32 six months post-vaccination.
Interpretation and novelty
The case demonstrates that MOGAD can present as ADEM in a previously healthy adult following COVID-19 vaccination, offering a rare example of this association.
Scientific and public-health significance
This case underscores the need for vigilance in monitoring rare neurological complications following vaccination, particularly in individuals without prior autoimmune conditions.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is limited to a single case, which may not be representative of broader trends, and the evidence is based on clinical observations rather than controlled trials, resulting in moderate confidence.

结构与突变分析揭示广谱严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2受体结合域识别的不同分子途径

Structural and mutational analyses define distinct molecular routes to broad SARS-CoV-2 receptor-binding domain recognition
作者： Morgan E. Abernathy, William B. Foreman, Jasmyn A. Lopez, Viren A. Baharani, Danielle Vahdat, Yu E. Lee, Michael R. Eso, Zijun Wang, Paul D. Bieniasz, Michel C Nussenzweig
摘要中文翻译摘要 由严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2感染或疫苗接种诱导的广泛反应性抗体可揭示保守的病毒脆弱性,并为具有广泛冠状病毒覆盖范围的疫苗提供信息。本研究表征了两种人源单克隆抗体B2014和C5078,它们识别严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2受体结合域(RBD)上的保守表位,并在抗原性不同的变异株中保持活性。值得注意的是,C5078还可识别多种刺突蛋白属病毒,并对目前流行的变异株(包括XFG和NB.1.8.1)保持活性。冷冻电子显微镜结构分析显示,B2014识别的表位邻近第3类抗体位点,而C5078靶向高度保守的隐蔽位点V表位。结构分析阐明了C5078如何利用亲和力成熟的相互作用结合保守的RBD残基,为其卓越广谱性提供分子基础。通过多种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株背景的深度突变扫描,进一步界定了抗体逃逸的潜在途径,解释了B2014在抗原性进化变异株中活性丧失的原因,同时揭示了C5078逃逸的高屏障。这些发现共同界定了广谱RBD识别的不同结构解决方案,并突出了可作为疫苗靶标的保守、突变受限表位,这类疫苗可能诱导对当前严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 进化及未来刺突蛋白属病毒出现具有韧性的抗体应答。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
该研究旨在通过分析针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的广泛反应性抗体,识别可为具有广泛冠状病毒覆盖范围的疫苗提供信息的保守病毒脆弱性。
设计与对象
本研究表征了两种人源单克隆抗体B2014和C5078,它们识别严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2受体结合域(RBD)上的保守表位,并在抗原性不同的变异株中保持活性。
核心方法
采用冷冻电子显微镜结构分析揭示B2014和C5078识别的表位,并通过多种严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株背景的深度突变扫描界定抗体逃逸的潜在途径。
主要结果
C5078靶向高度保守的隐蔽位点V表位,并利用亲和力成熟的相互作用结合保守的RBD残基,而B2014识别的表位邻近第3类抗体位点。研究发现B2014在抗原性进化变异株中活性丧失,但C5078具有高逃逸屏障。
解释与创新
研究结果为C5078的卓越广谱性提供分子基础,并界定了广谱RBD识别的不同结构解决方案,可指导针对当前严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 进化及未来刺突蛋白属病毒出现的疫苗设计。
科研与公卫意义
这些结果突出了可作为疫苗靶标的保守、突变受限表位,这类疫苗可能诱导对当前严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 进化及未来刺突蛋白属病毒出现具有韧性的抗体应答。
局限与证据强度
该研究基于单克隆抗体的结构与突变分析,可能无法充分代表自然群体中免疫应答的多样性,且证据源自摘要数据,限制了分析深度。

Structural and mutational analyses define distinct molecular routes to broad SARS-CoV-2 receptor-binding domain recognition

Authors: Morgan E. Abernathy, William B. Foreman, Jasmyn A. Lopez, Viren A. Baharani, Danielle Vahdat, Yu E. Lee, Michael R. Eso, Zijun Wang, Paul D. Bieniasz, Michel C Nussenzweig
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Broadly reactive antibodies elicited by SARS-CoV-2 infection or vaccination can reveal conserved viral vulnerabilities and inform vaccines with broad coronavirus coverage. Here, we characterize two human-derived monoclonal antibodies, B2014 and C5078, that recognize conserved epitopes on the SARS-CoV-2 RBD and retain activity across antigenically distinct variants. Notably, C5078 also recognizes diverse sarbecoviruses and remains active against currently circulating variants, including XFG and NB.1.8.1. Cryo-EM structures reveal that B2014 recognizes an epitope adjacent to the class 3 antibody site, whereas C5078 targets the highly conserved, cryptic site V epitope. Structural analysis defines how C5078 uses affinity-matured interactions to engage conserved RBD residues, providing a molecular basis for its exceptional breadth. Deep mutational scanning across multiple SARS-CoV-2 variant backgrounds further defines potential pathways of antibody escape, explaining the loss of B2014 activity against antigenically evolved variants while revealing a high barrier to escape from C5078. Together, these findings define distinct structural solutions for broad RBD recognition and highlight conserved, mutationally constrained epitopes that may serve as targets for vaccines designed to elicit antibody responses resilient to ongoing SARS-CoV-2 evolution and future sarbecovirus emergence.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to identify conserved viral vulnerabilities that could inform vaccines with broad coronavirus coverage by analyzing broadly reactive antibodies against SARS-CoV-2.
Design and population
The research characterized two human-derived monoclonal antibodies, B2014 and C5078, that recognize conserved epitopes on the SARS-CoV-2 receptor-binding domain (RBD) and retain activity across antigenically distinct variants.
Core methods
Cryo-EM structures were used to reveal the epitopes recognized by B2014 and C5078, and deep mutational scanning across multiple SARS-CoV-2 variant backgrounds was conducted to define potential pathways of antibody escape.
Main results
C5078 targets a highly conserved, cryptic site V epitope and uses affinity-matured interactions to engage conserved RBD residues, while B2014 recognizes an epitope adjacent to the class 3 antibody site. The study found that B2014 activity is lost against antigenically evolved variants, but C5078 has a high barrier to escape.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings provide a molecular basis for the exceptional breadth of C5078 and define distinct structural solutions for broad RBD recognition, which could guide vaccine design against SARS-CoV-2 evolution and future sarbecovirus emergence.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results highlight conserved, mutationally constrained epitopes that may serve as targets for vaccines designed to elicit antibody responses resilient to ongoing SARS-CoV-2 evolution and future sarbecovirus emergence.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on structural and mutational analyses of monoclonal antibodies, which may not fully represent the diversity of immune responses in natural populations, and the evidence is derived from abstract-only data, limiting the depth of analysis.

COVID-19 outcomes among vaccinated and unvaccinated patients receiving disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs and biologic therapies: a retrospective multihospital cohort study.

COVID-19 outcomes among vaccinated and unvaccinated patients receiving disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs and biologic therapies: a retrospective multihospital cohort study.
作者： Dat Le, Yijin Wert
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Patients receiving disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs (DMARDs) and biologic therapies were underrepresented in initial COVID-19 vaccine trials, limiting understanding of vaccine effectiveness in immunosuppressed populations.
设计与对象
A retrospective multihospital cohort study was conducted across seven hospitals within the UPMC health system in Central Pennsylvania. Adults (≥ 18 years) hospitalized with COVID-19 between March 2020 and April 2025 and receiving immunomodulatory therapy were included.
核心方法
通过电子健康记录和与注册数据库关联的编码数据确定疫苗接种状态,并评估了疫苗类型和严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株。采用多变量逻辑回归和伽马回归模型调整了关键的人口统计学和临床协变量。
主要结果
在254名患者中,144名接受了疫苗接种,110名未接种。接种疫苗的患者中位住院时间较短(6天 vs. 10天;p < 0.001),住院期间死亡率显著降低(6.25% vs 27.27%)。疫苗接种与调整后的死亡风险减少了87%相关。
解释与创新
The authors interpreted that among hospitalized patients receiving DMARDs and biologic therapies, COVID-19 vaccination was independently associated with reduced mortality and shorter hospital length of stay, supporting vaccination as a critical protective strategy in immunosuppressed populations.
科研与公卫意义
The findings support COVID-19 vaccination as protective in patients receiving DMARDs and biologic therapies, providing important evidence for clinical decision-making and public health strategies targeting immunosuppressed individuals.
局限与证据强度
尽管免疫原性研究显示抗体水平降低,但基于真实世界结局的证据仍有限。本研究是一项回顾性队列研究研究,可能引入偏倚并限制因果推断。

COVID-19 outcomes among vaccinated and unvaccinated patients receiving disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs and biologic therapies: a retrospective multihospital cohort study.

Authors: Dat Le, Yijin Wert
Original abstract (English)
Introduction/objectives Patients receiving disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs (DMARDs) and biologic therapies were underrepresented in initial COVID-19 vaccine trials, limiting understanding of vaccine effectiveness in immunosuppressed populations. While immunogenicity studies demonstrate reduced antibody, real-world outcome-based evidence remains limited. We evaluated the association between COVID-19 vaccination and clinical outcomes among hospitalized patients receiving systemic DMARDs and biologic therapies. Methods We conducted a retrospective cohort study across seven hospitals within the UPMC health system in Central Pennsylvania. Adults (≥ 18 years) hospitalized with COVID-19 between March 2020 and April 2025 and receiving immunomodulatory therapy. Vaccination status was determined using electronic health records and registry-linked coding data, with vaccine type and SARS-CoV-2 variants also assessed. The primary outcome was hospital length of stay (LOS). The secondary outcome was in-hospital mortality. Multivariable logistic regression and gamma regression models adjusted for key demographic and clinical covariates. Results Among 254 patients, 144 were vaccinated and 110 were unvaccinated. One hundred twenty-eight vaccinated patients received at least one booster. Vaccinated had shorter median LOS (6 vs. 10 days; p Conclusion Among hospitalized receiving DMARDs and biologic therapies, COVID-19 vaccination was independently associated with reduced mortality and shorter hospital length of stay, supporting vaccination as a critical protective strategy in immunosuppressed populations. Key Points • COVID-19 vaccination was independently associated with a 37% reduction in hospital length of stay among immunosuppressed patients receiving DMARDs or biologics. • Vaccinated patients experienced substantially lower in-hospital mortality (6.25% vs 27.27%), even after adjustment for clinical covariates. • Vaccination was associated with an 87% reduction in adjusted odds of death. • Findings support COVID-19 vaccination as protective in patients receiving DMARDs and biologic therapies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Patients receiving disease-modifying antirheumatic drugs (DMARDs) and biologic therapies were underrepresented in initial COVID-19 vaccine trials, limiting understanding of vaccine effectiveness in immunosuppressed populations.
Design and population
A retrospective multihospital cohort study was conducted across seven hospitals within the UPMC health system in Central Pennsylvania. Adults (≥ 18 years) hospitalized with COVID-19 between March 2020 and April 2025 and receiving immunomodulatory therapy were included.
Core methods
Vaccination status was determined using electronic health records and registry-linked coding data, with vaccine type and SARS-CoV-2 variants also assessed. Multivariable logistic regression and gamma regression models adjusted for key demographic and clinical covariates.
Main results
Among 254 patients, 144 were vaccinated and 110 were unvaccinated. Vaccinated patients had shorter median hospital length of stay (6 vs. 10 days; p < 0.001) and substantially lower in-hospital mortality (6.25% vs 27.27%). Vaccination was associated with an 87% reduction in adjusted odds of death.
Interpretation and novelty
The authors interpreted that among hospitalized patients receiving DMARDs and biologic therapies, COVID-19 vaccination was independently associated with reduced mortality and shorter hospital length of stay, supporting vaccination as a critical protective strategy in immunosuppressed populations.
Scientific and public-health significance
The findings support COVID-19 vaccination as protective in patients receiving DMARDs and biologic therapies, providing important evidence for clinical decision-making and public health strategies targeting immunosuppressed individuals.
Limitations and evidence strength
While immunogenicity studies demonstrate reduced antibody, real-world outcome-based evidence remains limited. The study is a retrospective cohort study, which may introduce bias and limit causal inference.

抗体介导的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2刺突蛋白受体结合域构象调控与入侵

Antibody-mediated SARS-CoV-2 entry and conformational regulation
作者： Casey L. Kiyohara, Sheamin Kim, Wendy E. Thomas
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The study addressed why only some antibodies mediate antibody-mediated entry (AME) of SARS-CoV-2 into monocytes and macrophages, a process linked to severe COVID-19, despite the role of RBD conformational dynamics in viral entry.
设计与对象
本研究识别出四组针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 RBD的单克隆抗体(mAbs),它们分别靶向RBD上的独特表位,并检测其构象调控能力与AME能力。
核心方法
通过空间位阻冲突量化阐明各抗体组别在构象调控差异上的结构基础;采用THP1来源巨噬细胞入侵实验评估AME能力。
主要结果
部分抗体组别可介导进入THP1来源巨噬细胞,而其他组别则无此能力;此类入侵可被其他组别的抗体抑制。
解释与创新
研究结果表明,严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2抗体的RBD构象调控、表位特异性与AME能力之间存在关联,为AME机制及其潜在抑制策略提供了新的见解。
科研与公卫意义
理解这些特征有助于指导严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2疫苗与治疗药物的设计,避免诱导介导AME的抗体,并促进产生抑制AME及其下游炎症效应的抗体。
局限与证据强度
证据基于THP1来源巨噬细胞体外实验与单克隆抗体研究,可能无法完全反映体内环境或多克隆抗体应答的情况。

Antibody-mediated SARS-CoV-2 entry and conformational regulation

Authors: Casey L. Kiyohara, Sheamin Kim, Wendy E. Thomas
Original abstract (English)
ABSTRACT Antibody-mediated entry (AME) of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) into monocytes and macrophages has been linked to activation of inflammatory phenotypes that are associated with severe coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19), but why only some antibodies mediate this entry while others do not is unknown. However, it has been demonstrated that conformational dynamics of the SARS-CoV-2 receptor-binding domain (RBD) are critical to viral entry, and that antibodies targeting the RBD can conformationally regulate these dynamics. Here, we identified four groups of RBD-specific monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) that target unique epitopes on the SARS-CoV-2 RBD and are also associated with different RBD conformational regulation and AME abilities. Steric clash quantification elucidated a structural basis for differences in conformational regulation by these antibody groups. We found that some, but not all, antibody groups were able to mediate entry into THP1-derived macrophages, and also found that this entry was inhibitable by antibodies in other groups. Collectively, these results suggest that there is a connection between RBD conformational regulation, epitope, and AME for antibodies targeting SARS-CoV-2. This connection improves understanding of the AME mechanism and how to inhibit AME for SARS-CoV-2, which has potential applications in the continued development of safe and effective vaccines and therapeutics for COVID-19 and related diseases. IMPORTANCE Antibody-mediated entry (AME) of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2) into immune cells and resulting activation of inflammatory pathways is increasingly being associated with coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19) disease severity. Vaccination against COVID-19 remains one of our strongest tools to prevent these effects; vaccines continue to protect patients from both severe and long COVID-19 symptoms, and serum from vaccinated individuals was shown not to cause AME in immune cells in vitro. However, a lack of understanding about why some, but not all, antibodies cause AME limits our ability to actively prevent AME in antigen design. In this work, we identify characteristics that differentiate individual AME and non-AME antibodies. We also report that AME can be inhibited by other antibodies. Understanding these characteristics and differences between antibodies could allow us to add functionality to already effective vaccines by designing them to actively avoid eliciting AME antibodies and promote antibodies that inhibit AME and its downstream systemic effects. We also report that AME can be inhibited by other antibodies.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study addressed why only some antibodies mediate antibody-mediated entry (AME) of SARS-CoV-2 into monocytes and macrophages, a process linked to severe COVID-19, despite the role of RBD conformational dynamics in viral entry.
Design and population
The research identified four groups of RBD-specific monoclonal antibodies (mAbs) targeting unique epitopes on the SARS-CoV-2 RBD and examined their conformational regulation and AME abilities.
Core methods
Steric clash quantification was used to elucidate structural differences in conformational regulation by the antibody groups, and entry assays were performed using THP1-derived macrophages to assess AME.
Main results
Some antibody groups mediated entry into THP1-derived macrophages, while others did not, and entry was inhibitable by antibodies from other groups.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings suggest a connection between RBD conformational regulation, epitope specificity, and AME for SARS-CoV-2 antibodies, providing new insights into the mechanism and potential inhibition of AME.
Scientific and public-health significance
Understanding these characteristics could inform vaccine and therapeutic design to avoid eliciting AME antibodies and promote those that inhibit AME and its downstream inflammatory effects.
Limitations and evidence strength
The evidence is limited to in vitro assays using THP1-derived macrophages and monoclonal antibodies, which may not fully reflect in vivo conditions or polyclonal antibody responses.

解离动力学与亲和力门控:HR2茎螺旋抗体对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的中和作用

Dissociation kinetics and avidity gate SARS-CoV-2 neutralization by HR2 stem helix antibodies
作者： Virginia Crivelli, Concetta Guerra, Morgan E. Abernathy, Jacopo Sgrignani, Giada Zoppi, Mia L. Greeson, Annalisa Sanga, Patrizia Locatelli, Jasmine Cantergiani, Benedetta Cena
摘要中文翻译摘要 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 已使多种临床单克隆抗体的疗效下降,凸显出针对病毒保守区域的治疗药物的迫切需求。刺突蛋白(S)七聚体重复序列2(HR2)茎螺旋在严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株及相关β冠状病毒中高度保守。尽管针对此区域的抗体可中和感染,但其自然发生率及严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2仍鲜有了解。我们此前已鉴定出针对该区域内一段保守肽段(HR2冷点)的人源中和抗体。本研究表明,即使在反复抗原暴露后,血浆IgG对该区域的反应性仍罕见。为期30个月的纵向分析显示,HR2特异性抗体持续发生体细胞超突变,但无一超过hr2.016的中和效价或广谱性,后者在初次感染后不久即出现。四种HR2茎螺旋抗体的晶体结构揭示了不同抗体系的收敛性识别模式。将hr2.016与其非中和性克隆同源抗体hr2.086比较后发现,结构收敛性掩盖了其结合动力学的差异。表面等离子共振与分子动力学模拟显示,hr2.016具有更稳定的相互作用网络,解离更缓慢且S蛋白滞留时间更长。中和作用需IgG格式,提示存在亲和力驱动的机制。综上,本研究界定了HR2茎螺旋处中和作用的动力学与亲和力约束,并将hr2.016定位为具韧性的治疗候选药物。
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
本研究旨在探索针对严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2刺突蛋白(S)七聚体重复序列2(HR2)茎螺旋———一段保守区域———的抗体中和潜力,因临床单克隆抗体对不断进化的严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株疗效已下降。
设计与对象
本研究采用纵向分析方法,在为期30个月内追踪血浆IgG对HR2茎螺旋的反应性,研究对象为反复暴露于抗原的个体。
核心方法
研究者开展了四种HR2茎螺旋抗体的晶体结构分析,并利用表面等离子共振与分子动力学模拟评估结合动力学与相互作用稳定性。
主要结果
纵向分析显示HR2特异性抗体持续发生体细胞超突变,但无一超过hr2.016的中和效价或广谱性。hr2.016与其非中和性克隆同源抗体hr2.086间观察到结构收敛性,但其结合动力学存在差异。
解释与创新
研究结果强调了HR2茎螺旋处中和作用的动力学与亲和力约束,并表明hr2.016因其稳定的相互作用网络与缓慢解离特性,可能成为具韧性的治疗候选药物。
科研与公卫意义
本研究结果为开发针对病毒保守区域的治疗药物提供了见解,有助于应对未来严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2变异株并改善中和策略。
局限与证据强度
本研究基于摘要证据,纵向分析的可推广性可能受限。由于依赖结构与动力学数据而缺乏详细临床结局,证据强度为中等。

Dissociation kinetics and avidity gate SARS-CoV-2 neutralization by HR2 stem helix antibodies

Authors: Virginia Crivelli, Concetta Guerra, Morgan E. Abernathy, Jacopo Sgrignani, Giada Zoppi, Mia L. Greeson, Annalisa Sanga, Patrizia Locatelli, Jasmine Cantergiani, Benedetta Cena
Original abstract (English)
Abstract SARS-CoV-2 evolution has reduced the efficacy of clinical monoclonal antibodies, underscoring the need for therapeutics targeting conserved viral regions. The Spike (S) heptad repeat 2 (HR2) stem helix is highly conserved across SARS-CoV-2 variants and related betacoronaviruses. Although antibodies to this region can neutralize infection, their natural occurrence and evolution remain poorly understood. We previously identified human neutralizing antibodies to a conserved peptide within this region (HR2 coldspot). Here, we show that plasma IgG reactivity to this region remains rare, even after repeated antigen exposure. Longitudinal analysis over 30 months revealed continued somatic hypermutation of HR2-specific antibodies, yet none surpassed the potency or breadth of hr2.016, which emerged shortly after primary infection. Crystal structures of four HR2 stem helix antibodies revealed convergent recognition across distinct antibody lineages. Comparison of hr2.016 with its non-neutralizing clonal relative hr2.086 showed that structural convergence masks distinct binding kinetics. Surface plasmon resonance and molecular dynamics simulations revealed a more stable interaction network for hr2.016, with slower dissociation and prolonged S residence time. Neutralization required the IgG format, supporting an avidity-driven mechanism. Together, these findings define kinetic and avidity constraints governing neutralization at the HR2 stem helix and position hr2.016 as a resilient therapeutic candidate.
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
The study aimed to understand the neutralization potential of antibodies targeting the SARS-CoV-2 Spike (S) heptad repeat 2 (HR2) stem helix, a conserved region, given the reduced efficacy of clinical monoclonal antibodies against evolving SARS-CoV-2 variants.
Design and population
The study involved longitudinal analysis of plasma IgG reactivity to the HR2 stem helix over 30 months, focusing on individuals with repeated antigen exposure.
Core methods
The researchers conducted crystal structure analysis of four HR2 stem helix antibodies and used surface plasmon resonance and molecular dynamics simulations to assess binding kinetics and interaction stability.
Main results
Longitudinal analysis showed continued somatic hypermutation of HR2-specific antibodies, but none exceeded the potency or breadth of hr2.016. Structural convergence was observed between hr2.016 and its non-neutralizing clonal relative hr2.086, yet distinct binding kinetics were noted.
Interpretation and novelty
The findings highlight kinetic and avidity constraints in neutralization at the HR2 stem helix and suggest that hr2.016 could be a resilient therapeutic candidate due to its stable interaction network and slower dissociation.
Scientific and public-health significance
These results provide insights into the development of therapeutics targeting conserved viral regions, which could be crucial for combating future SARS-CoV-2 variants and improving neutralization strategies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study is based on abstract-only evidence, and the longitudinal analysis may have limited generalizability. The evidence strength is moderate due to the reliance on structural and kinetic data without detailed clinical outcomes.

Prognostic impact of right ventricular dilatation and echocardiography use criteria in hospitalized COVID-19 patients.

Prognostic impact of right ventricular dilatation and echocardiography use criteria in hospitalized COVID-19 patients.
作者： Arnold Méndez-Toro, Ingrid Tatiana Rojas-Ruiz, Luis Eduardo Silva-DiazGranados, Andrés Ruano-Cadena, Manuel Agustín Paz-Meneses, Ricardo Andrés Novoa-Alvarez
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
The surge of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 [coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19)] infection created unprecedented diagnostic and logistical pressures. Given the established potential for cardiac injury and the critical need to optimize scarce resources, establishing the clinical utility and prognostic value of transthoracic echocardiography (TTE) became imperative.
设计与对象
We performed a retrospective cohort study analyzing records from patients with confirmed COVID-19 who underwent TTE. Total 149 patients were analyzed. Median age was 66 years [interquartile range (IQR): 56-73], median hospital stay was 13 days (IQR: 6-23).
核心方法
收集了社会人口学、生化和超声心动图参数。死亡率是主要结局。我们评估了TTE指征的观察者间一致性(Kappa统计量),依据美国心脏病学会基金会2011年和美国超声心动学会2020年的指南,并评估了临床影响的确定(后续患者管理的改变)。
主要结果
总体和重症监护单元死亡率发生率分别为39.6%和60%。升高的生化标志物(白细胞、中性粒细胞、乳酸脱氢酶和C反应蛋白)与死亡率相关。关键的是,右心室(RV)扩张和/或应变(P = 0.008)被确定为唯一显著预测死亡率的超声心动图发现。对AUC分类的观察者间一致性较高(κ ≥ 0.798)。此外,TTE在79.7%的病例中促使了临床管理的改变。
解释与创新
RV pathology is a potent, quantifiable prognostic indicator. While AUC demonstrated high reliability, the significant and frequent changes in management suggest that the current guidelines may possess inherent limitations when addressing the unique, acute prognostic complexities of severe COVID-19 disease.
科研与公卫意义
The findings can cautiously inform the use of echocardiography in the assessment of prognosis for hospitalized COVID-19 patients. They also highlight the need for updated guidelines to better address the acute prognostic complexities of severe disease.
局限与证据强度
该研究是回顾性的,基于记录,这可能会引入偏倚并限制建立因果关系的能力。

Prognostic impact of right ventricular dilatation and echocardiography use criteria in hospitalized COVID-19 patients.

Authors: Arnold Méndez-Toro, Ingrid Tatiana Rojas-Ruiz, Luis Eduardo Silva-DiazGranados, Andrés Ruano-Cadena, Manuel Agustín Paz-Meneses, Ricardo Andrés Novoa-Alvarez
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: The surge of severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 [coronavirus disease 2019 (COVID-19)] infection created unprecedented diagnostic and logistical pressures. Given the established potential for cardiac injury and the critical need to optimize scarce resources, establishing the clinical utility and prognostic value of transthoracic echocardiography (TTE) became imperative. AIM: To evaluate the prognostic predictors and the adherence to appropriate use criteria (AUC) for TTE in a highly affected setting. METHODS: We performed a retrospective cohort study analyzing records from patients with confirmed COVID-19 who underwent TTE. Socio-demographic, biochemical, and echocardiographic parameters were collected. Mortality was the primary outcome. We assessed inter-observer agreement (Kappa statistic) for TTE indications (based on American College of Cardiology Foundation 2011 and American Society of Echocardiography 2020 guidelines) and the determination of clinical impact (a subsequent change in patient management). RESULTS: Total 149 patients were analyzed. Median age was 66 years [interquartile range (IQR): 56-73], median hospital stay was 13 days (IQR: 6-23). Overall and intensive care unit mortality rates were 39.6% and 60%, respectively. Elevated biochemical markers (leukocytes, neutrophils, lactate dehydrogenase, and C-reactive protein) were associated with mortality. Crucially, right ventricular (RV) dilatation and/or strain (P = 0.008) was identified as the sole echocardiographic finding significantly predictive of mortality. Inter-observer agreement for classifying AUC was high (κ ≥ 0.798). Furthermore, TTE prompted a change in clinical management in 79.7% of the cases. CONCLUSION: RV pathology is a potent, quantifiable prognostic indicator. While AUC demonstrated high reliability, the significant and frequent changes in management suggest that the current guidelines may possess inherent limitations when addressing the unique, acute prognostic complexities of severe COVID-19 disease.
View research seven-element analysis

长新冠 所致心血管、肾脏及肺部风险:按年龄和性别分层的回顾性 队列研究 研究

Cardiovascular, Renal, and Pulmonary Risks of Long COVID: A Retrospective Cohort Study Stratified by Age and Sex.
作者： Akash Pradeep, Izola Ramalho, Martha L. Carvour, Srija M Sivamurugan, Upinder Singh, Alejandro P. Comellas, Sanjana Dayal, Srija Manchkanti
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
A substantial proportion of patients with acute COVID-19 develop postacute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 infection (Long COVID). The risk of adverse cardiovascular and related outcomes in Long COVID remains elusive.
设计与对象
We performed a retrospective cohort study using the global TriNetX network (>150 million patients). Adults with documented COVID-19 were classified by the presence/absence of clinically recognized Long COVID.
核心方法
我们分析了 15 项 CRP 结局的绝对风险和相对风险,按年龄(18-50 岁、51-64 岁、≥65 岁)分层。在排除既往相关结局患者后,采用倾向性评分匹配调整年龄、性别及常见混杂因素。
主要结果
Among 2 613 432 adults identified with COVID-19 within TriNetX network, 315 612 matched individuals were included in the study. Long COVID was associated with higher risk of most CRP outcomes across all ages regardless of sex. Relative risks were disproportionately higher in younger adults, especially in young women for cardiovascular and renal outcomes and in young men for pulmonary outcomes.
解释与创新
We hypothesized that individuals with Long COVID are at elevated risk for adverse cardiovascular, renal, and pulmonary (CRP) outcomes compared with those who recovered from COVID-19 without developing Long COVID. Findings remained directionally consistent across sensitivity analyses.
科研与公卫意义
这些发现支持对 长新冠 患者继续进行 监测 及心血管及相关疾病风险降低策略的必要性。既往 严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2 疫苗接种 未持续与 CRP 风险降低相关。
局限与证据强度
本研究未报告具体的研究设计局限性。由于研究为回顾性设计且尽管采用倾向性评分匹配仍可能存在混杂因素,对研究结果的信心度为中等。

Cardiovascular, Renal, and Pulmonary Risks of Long COVID: A Retrospective Cohort Study Stratified by Age and Sex.

Authors: Akash Pradeep, Izola Ramalho, Martha L. Carvour, Srija M Sivamurugan, Upinder Singh, Alejandro P. Comellas, Sanjana Dayal, Srija Manchkanti
Original abstract (English)
BACKGROUND: A substantial proportion of patients with acute COVID-19 develop postacute sequelae of SARS-CoV-2 infection (Long COVID). The risk of adverse cardiovascular and related outcomes in Long COVID remains elusive. We hypothesized that individuals with Long COVID are at elevated risk for adverse cardiovascular, renal, and pulmonary (CRP) outcomes compared with those who recovered from COVID-19 without developing Long COVID. METHODS: We performed a retrospective cohort study using the global TriNetX network (>150 million patients). Adults with documented COVID-19 were classified by the presence/absence of clinically recognized Long COVID. We analyzed absolute risks and relative risks for 15 CRP outcomes, stratified by age (18-50, 51-64, ≥65 years). After excluding patients with preexisting outcomes of interest, propensity score matching was applied for age, sex, and common confounders. RESULTS: Among 2 613 432 adults identified with COVID-19 within TriNetX network, 315 612 matched individuals were included in the study. Long COVID was associated with higher risk of most CRP outcomes across all ages regardless of sex. Relative risks were disproportionately higher in younger adults, especially in young women for cardiovascular and renal outcomes and in young men for pulmonary outcomes. Findings remained directionally consistent across sensitivity analyses. Prior SARS-CoV-2 vaccination was not consistently associated with reduced CRP risk. CONCLUSIONS: Clinically recognized Long COVID was associated with increased risk of CRP outcomes, with relatively higher relative risks observed in younger adults. These findings support the need for continuing surveillance and risk-reduction strategies for cardiovascular and related disorders in Long COVID.
View research seven-element analysis

Main results

The supplied evidence does not report specific limitations of the study design. Confidence in the findings is moderate due to the retrospective nature of the study and potential for confounding factors despite propensity score matching.

一项针对日本6至11岁感染严重急性呼吸综合征冠状病毒2的参与者使用恩赛特韦的3期研究

A phase 3 Study of ensitrelvir in japanese participants aged 6–11 years with SARS-CoV-2 infection
作者： Takashi Nakano, Hiroyuki Moriuchi, Isao Miyairi, Takumi Imamura, Maria Matsumoto, Yuko Tsuge, Takeki Uehara
摘要中文翻译
查看研究七要素
问题与背景
Children under 12 years with COVID-19 have limited antiviral treatment options due to age or bodyweight restrictions for currently approved oral antivirals, creating an unmet clinical need.
设计与对象
This phase 3, double-blind, placebo-controlled study randomized participants aged 6 to under 12 years with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 in Japan to receive ensitrelvir or placebo once daily for 5 days.
核心方法
参与者以2:1比例随机分配至恩赛特韦组(基于体重给药)或安慰剂组,主要终点为安全性和血浆浓度-时间曲线评估。
主要结果
在117名随机分配参与者中(中位年龄10岁),78名接受恩赛特韦,39名接受安慰剂;恩赛特韦在第4天时病毒RNA较安慰剂降低约1.0 log10拷贝/毫升(95%置信区间,-1.45至-0.47)。
解释与创新
作者得出结论:恩赛特韦在该儿科人群中显示出良好的安全性特征、与成人匹配的血浆浓度-时间曲线,且病毒RNA降低幅度优于安慰剂。
科研与公卫意义
Ensitrelvir may offer a potential treatment option for pediatric patients aged 6 to under 12 years with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 who are currently underserved by existing antiviral therapies.
局限与证据强度
该研究样本量较小且症状缓解时间无统计学显著改善,证据强度有限,需谨慎解读其临床意义。

A phase 3 Study of ensitrelvir in japanese participants aged 6–11 years with SARS-CoV-2 infection

Authors: Takashi Nakano, Hiroyuki Moriuchi, Isao Miyairi, Takumi Imamura, Maria Matsumoto, Yuko Tsuge, Takeki Uehara
Original abstract (English)
Abstract Background Children aged <12 years with COVID-19 have limited treatment options; currently approved oral antivirals are restricted by age or bodyweight. We evaluated the safety and plasma concentration-time profiles and exploratory efficacy of ensitrelvir, a once-daily oral 3C-like protease inhibitor, in children with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 in Japan. Methods This phase 3, double-blind, placebo-controlled study randomized (2:1) participants aged 6 to <12 years presenting ≤72 h after COVID-19 onset to receive ensitrelvir (bodyweight-based dosing) or placebo once daily for 5 days. Primary endpoints were safety and PK. Secondary endpoints included change in viral RNA and time to resolution (TTR) of key COVID-19 symptoms (stuffy or runny nose and cough) plus fever. Results Of 117 randomized participants (median age 10 years), 78 received ensitrelvir and 39 received placebo. Ensitrelvir was well tolerated with no new safety concerns. Plasma concentration-time profiles were similar across bodyweight categories (20 to <30, 30 to <40, and ≥40 kg). Ensitrelvir reduced viral RNA vs placebo on Day 4, by approximately 1.0 log10 copies/mL (95% CI, –1.45 to –0.47). Median TTR of key COVID-19 symptoms was 140.5 h with ensitrelvir and 146.2 h with placebo (median difference –5.7 h [95% CI, –73.6 to 43.4 h]). Conclusions In children aged 6 to <12 years with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 treated with ensitrelvir, we observed a favorable safety profile, adult-matched plasma concentration-time profile, and a greater reduction in viral RNA vs placebo. Ensitrelvir represents a potential treatment option for underserved pediatric patients with mild-to-moderate COVID-19. Clinical Trial Registration Japan Registry of Clinical Trials, jRCT2031230140 (https://jrct.mhlw.go.jp/en-latest-detail/jRCT2031230140)
View research seven-element analysis
Question and background
Children under 12 years with COVID-19 have limited antiviral treatment options due to age or bodyweight restrictions for currently approved oral antivirals, creating an unmet clinical need.
Design and population
This phase 3, double-blind, placebo-controlled study randomized participants aged 6 to under 12 years with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 in Japan to receive ensitrelvir or placebo once daily for 5 days.
Core methods
Participants were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to ensitrelvir (bodyweight-based dosing) or placebo, with primary endpoints assessing safety and plasma concentration-time profiles.
Main results
Among 117 randomized participants (median age 10 years), 78 received ensitrelvir and 39 received placebo; ensitrelvir reduced viral RNA by approximately 1.0 log10 copies/mL versus placebo on Day 4 (95% CI, –1.45 to –0.47).
Interpretation and novelty
The authors concluded that ensitrelvir demonstrated a favorable safety profile, adult-matched plasma concentration-time profiles, and a greater reduction in viral RNA compared with placebo in this pediatric population.
Scientific and public-health significance
Ensitrelvir may offer a potential treatment option for pediatric patients aged 6 to under 12 years with mild-to-moderate COVID-19 who are currently underserved by existing antiviral therapies.
Limitations and evidence strength
The study’s small sample size and lack of statistically significant improvement in symptom resolution time limit the strength of evidence, warranting cautious interpretation of clinical significance.
